## Code build (Next.js), built 28 Oct 2026
The user said "Let's just build from here". I read that as: build the real landing page in code from the approved Figma designs. No repository was linked (the workspace has no GitHub account attached), so I announced and used this assumption: Next.js App Router with TypeScript and plain CSS. If the user's repo uses something else, port the components and CSS (the structure is simple).

- Location: /home/claude/akines-landing in the workspace. Zip with everything except node_modules and .next: /home/claude/akines-landing.zip (clean `npm ci && npm run build` verified from the zip). README.md in the project explains running, structure, behaviour, launch checklist and deviations.
- Run: `npm install`, `npm run dev` (port 3000), `npm run build`, `npm start`, `npm run lint` (tsc --noEmit). No environment variables are needed (NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL was removed on 29 Oct when the CTAs were re-pointed to the 404 page).
- Stack: Next 15.5, React 19, TypeScript 5.9, plain CSS (src/styles/tokens.css plus one file per section), @fontsource Alice 400 and Manrope 400/500/600, lucide-react, next/image. `npm audit` flags PostCSS inside Next 15 (build time only); fix by moving to Next 16 later.
- Breakpoints: mobile under 768 (design 402), tablet 768 to 1279 (design 1194), desktop 1280 and up (design 1512), container max 1512. In-between fixes: trust chips two columns 768 to 1179 then four; footer brand beside columns from 1140 and wide columns from 1440.
- Files: src/content/content.ts (all copy, image paths, data; `concept: true` flag for experiences not yet live), src/lib/links.ts (every destination; privacy, terms, accessibility and guidelines are `#` placeholders, contact scrolls to the footer, sign in, join and philosophy originally went to the app's /login and /about and now all go to /404, see the 404 section), src/components (Header, Menu, Hero, Intention, Rhythm, Experiences, Trust, Community, FinalCta, Footer, Button, Logo, Icon, Lines, DiscoveryLink), public/images (27 JPEGs: all AI placeholders except the user's hero photo, including the two 404 photographs added 29 Oct; community and closing exist as tight crops for tablet and up plus `-full` versions for mobile because Figma's mobile uses the full frame).
- Behaviour built: header clear then solid after the hero (64/76/80 px solid), hiding on scroll down and returning on scroll up (added 29 Oct), menu (frosted panel from 768, full-screen modal on mobile with Join and Sign in, scroll links, scroll lock, focus trap, Escape, focus return), how-it-works stepper (prev/next wrap, row click, keyboard, crossfade, reserved height so the page never jumps), trust carousel (12 s auto-advance with loader in the active chip, changed from 6 s on 29 Oct; no pause on mouse hover (removed on 29 Oct at the user's request after briefly being added); pauses only on keyboard focus or with the pause button, which shows on focus; reduced motion disables auto-advance), skip link, reduced-motion styles, and (added 29 Oct) the motion layer described below.
- Verification done: per-section mean brightness and layout compared with Figma screenshots at 1512, 1194 and 402 (section heights within 0 to 6 px; known exceptions: stepper list +59 to +63 px by design, hero fills the viewport height while Figma is a fixed frame); scrims match Figma stops (a cascade bug that made the cards too dark was fixed); button heights match Figma per breakpoint (tablet and mobile final CTA pills are 48, desktop 62); mobile photo crops for community and closing use the full frame; headline wrapping matches Figma on mobile (text-wrap pretty instead of balance, hero keeps balance); menu panel details match (close glyph alignment, 1.5 px chevrons, tablet logo size, tagline line height); scripted functional checks (menu on all three sizes, stepper, trust carousel, header state, overflow and console errors) all pass; no horizontal overflow from 360 to 2560 px; contrast of white text on photographs at least 5.4:1 against the brightest pixel under each text run, muted text on ivory 4.52:1 (token #6E6E6E).
- Bugs found and fixed while verifying: footer overflowed sideways between about 768 and 1400 px; trust chip labels wrapped at tablet width; stepper height differed by under 1 px between states; stage copy was not bottom-anchored above the chips.
- Known deviations and trade-offs: hero fills the viewport height; stepper list is as tall as its tallest state; secondary text darkened to #6E6E6E; on mobile the intention panels use CSS order so keyboard order is 01, 03, 02, 04; hover and focus states are simple additions (Figma has none); first load after a deploy can show the trust stage dark for a moment while the image optimizer warms its cache.
- Not done: analytics events from the PRD are not wired; legal pages and contact destination do not exist; images are placeholders.
- Next steps: confirm destinations in src/lib/links.ts, replace placeholder images (same names and aspect ratios), decide which experiences are live (concept tags), review the build in a browser on real devices, then connect analytics and deploy (Vercel works as is).

### Motion layer, added 29 Oct 2026
The user asked for interactive animations, scroll reveal animations for images and text, and other animations that make the site feel premium, then added staggered animations (several elements animating in sequence with a slight, overlapping delay instead of all at once). Built as a layer on top of the finished page; no component logic depends on it.
- Files: src/styles/motion.css (all keyframes and start states, imported last in layout.tsx), src/lib/motion.ts (plain DOM code: finds elements, tags them with data attributes, IntersectionObserver, hero scroll scrub, eased anchor scrolling; exports `initMotion` and `scrollToId`), src/components/Motion.tsx (client component that calls `initMotion` once, mounted at the end of the page). Also: `--ease-out` and `--ease-io` in tokens.css, rebuilt `.btn` and `.dlink` in base.css, underline draw-in on sign-in and footer links, a `words` prop on the `Lines` component (hero headline), exit animation in Menu.tsx (`data-closing`, 280 ms, then the section scroll starts), and an inline head script in layout.tsx that adds `js` to the html element before first paint and `hero-ready` when the hero photo has loaded (or after 4 s).
- Hero: photo fades and settles (scale 1.14 to 1 over 2.4 s) once loaded; header items, eyebrow, headline words (50 ms apart) and the two buttons arrive in sequence; scrolling out drifts and zooms the photo and lifts and fades the copy (CSS variable `--hp`, 0 to 1).
- Scroll reveals: three kinds set by `data-reveal`: `up` (text rises and fades), `words` (headline words rise one after another, 55 ms apart, via `<span class="w" style="--wi:n">`), `frame` (photographs and full-bleed sections open downwards behind an ivory curtain pseudo-element while the image settles from scale 1.16). Below-the-fold photos fade in when loaded (`data-loading`). A sweep reveals anything scrolled past unseen (fast flick, link jump).
- Stagger: elements that intersect in the same observer batch get `--rd` = index x 100 ms (max 8 steps), in reading order, counted per section or footer; animations last about 1.1 s so neighbours overlap. Frames reveal immediately (0 ms).
- Interaction: buttons fill with a rising sweep on hover and scale to 0.97 on press, `dlink` and footer or sign-in underlines redraw, panel/story/full-card photos zoom slowly on hover, trust chips lift, stepper rows and photos ease between states, trust stage copy lifts in with a delay, the menu panel, rules, links and actions animate in one by one and reverse on close, section links glide (duration min(1500, 650 + distance x 0.11) ms, easeInOutCubic, respects scroll-margin, stops on wheel, touch, key or mouse down).
- Safety: hidden start states exist only under the `js` class and `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`. With reduced motion `initMotion` returns before tagging anything, links jump, and layout is pixel-identical to the pre-motion build. Print shows everything. Hover effects are inside `@media (hover: hover)`. Only data attributes and CSS variables are set on elements (never classes), so React re-renders cannot undo them.
- Design decisions worth knowing: the frame reveal uses a curtain instead of `clip-path` because Chrome's IntersectionObserver treats content under an ancestor clip-path as not intersecting (tested); individual CSS transform properties (`scale`, `translate`) are used so reveal, hover and scroll effects compose without fighting over `transform`; reveal animations use `animation-fill-mode: backwards` so elements return to their natural state afterwards.
- To tweak: selector lists `UP`, `WORDS`, `FRAME` and constants `STAGGER_MS` (100) and `STAGGER_MAX` (8) at the top of motion.ts; durations and easing in motion.css.
- Verification: 51 of 51 original functional checks still pass; 23 of 23 motion checks (hero sequence, every element revealed after a slow scroll and after a jump, nothing left transparent, eased scroll shape and landing under the header, wheel interrupts the glide, hero scrub, menu exit, reduced motion tags nothing, mobile no overflow, no console errors or warnings); 9 of 9 stagger checks (hero words 50 ms apart, header items, headline words, community moments 0/100/200, trust chips and footer columns 100 ms apart, delays shorter than the duration so neighbours overlap). With reduced motion the page heights match the pre-motion build exactly (15546 at 1512, 12817 at 1194, 15016 at 402) with blurred pixel differences of at most 4 of 255.
- Known trade-offs: the first load after a deploy can show a photo fade in late while the image optimizer warms; parallax and curtain reveals add a little GPU work on very old phones (reduced motion switches everything off).

### 404 page and CTA links, added 29 Oct 2026
The user asked: (1) remove all links that send the CTA buttons to https://akines.vercel.app/login, and (2) make all CTAs link to a well designed and built 404 page with a full-screen "connecting" image, a link back to the home page and premium, staggered reveal animation.
- Links: src/lib/links.ts now has `export const NOT_FOUND = '/404'` and `signIn`, `join` and `philosophy` all use it, so every CTA (header and menu Sign in and Join Akines, hero and closing "Find my kind of connection", the experience and intention cards, "Explore the Akines philosophy", "Understand our approach", footer Sign in and Join) leads to /404. 19 links on the page point at /404. No link points at akines.vercel.app any more; NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL and .env.example were removed. Interpretation I took: "all CTA" includes the two philosophy text CTAs (they went to the live /about page). Unchanged on purpose: in-page scroll links (menu, "Discover Akines", "Explore Akines"), the contact link, and the footer legal links (`#` placeholders; they are not CTAs). To go live, put the real URL into the matching entry in links.ts.
- Page: src/app/not-found.tsx (server component; Next serves it for /404 and every unknown URL with HTTP 404 and noindex; title "Page not found | Akines"). Full-screen photograph via `<picture>` and `getImageProps`: public/images/notfound.jpg (1376x768, tablet and desktop) and notfound-portrait.jpg (768x1376, mobile). Both were generated with the Figma generate_image tool (model gemini-3.1-flash-image, two paid calls) in the Akines Figma file as nodes 16925:149 (landscape) and 16926:149 (portrait): two hands, one in a cream knit cuff and one in an olive linen cuff, reaching toward each other and about to touch, golden-hour park with bokeh. Layout: logo link top centre, then bottom-centred copy: "Error 404" label with two hairlines, headline "This connection has not been made yet.", body "The page you are looking for may have moved, or it is still being built. Let's take you back to where belonging begins.", button "Return home" (href "/"). Copy is in content.ts (`notFoundPage`).
- Styles and motion: src/styles/notfound.css (imported last in layout.tsx), src/components/NotFoundMotion.tsx (pointer parallax only). Entrance on load, staggered with overlapping delays (each about 1.1 s): photograph fades in and settles once loaded (hero-ready class from the head script, which now also listens for `nf__img`; 4 s fallback), scrim 1.6 s, logo 0.3 s, hairlines grow inward toward the label 0.5 s, label 0.6 s, headline words from 0.75 s at 60 ms apart, body 1.3 s, button 1.45 s; then the photograph drifts (scale 1 to 1.07 over 28 s) and on hover-capable devices follows the pointer by up to 14 px (CSS variables --mx and --my). Reduced motion: nothing animated or moving, everything visible immediately. Wrapper `.nf__parallax` is taller than the screen (inset -24px, and -96px above from 768 up) so the parallax never shows an edge and the copy clears the fingertips.
- Other change: the skip link moved from layout.tsx into page.tsx so the 404 page does not have a skip link to a missing anchor.
- Verification (Next.js build): 41 of 41 new checks (status 404, title, h1, Return home, logo link, landscape and portrait photograph choice, no external links, every CTA button and link is /404 or an in-page anchor, header Join, mobile menu Join and hero CTA all open it, animation order and 60 ms word cascade, overlap, hairlines grow inward, photograph fade and drift, everything fully visible at the end, pointer parallax, reduced motion, no overflow or console errors at 360, 402, 768, 1194, 1512, 1920 and 1280x600). Regressions still pass: 51 of 51 functional, 23 of 23 motion, 9 of 9 stagger. The only console message is the browser's own note that the document has status 404, which the test ignores.
- Preview artifact: single files cannot have routes, so the same 404 markup is a full-screen layer (`#nf-view`) that opens when the address hash is #not-found (a plain #name is allowed in artifacts). CTAs are `href="#not-found"`; from the mobile menu the menu closes first. Behaviours: page behind is inert and scroll locked, focus moves into the view and returns on Back, Return home and the logo hide it and scroll to the top, Back also closes it and keeps the scroll position, opening the link with #not-found shows it, the entrance replays on every open. The two photographs are published beside the page (the hidden view's image loads quietly at low priority so it is ready; it does not trigger the hero entrance). 23 of 23 checks pass on the preview, plus the earlier 55 functional (the carousel timing check got a wider margin), 22 of 23 motion (the one difference is the hash check, by design) and 9 of 9 stagger checks.
- Figma housekeeping: the two generated frames are still sitting in the Akines Figma file (nodes above); the image URLs from the tool expire after 7 days, but both images are saved in public/images.
- Open: the real destinations for sign in, join and the philosophy page; whether the philosophy CTAs should keep pointing at the live /about page instead; the footer legal link pages.

### Header Join hover, two-line trust headline and 12 second carousel, added 29 Oct 2026
The user sent two screenshots (header over the hero with an outlined "Join Akines" pill; the trust stage with its headline on four lines) and asked: (1) use the same hover animation as the other CTA buttons for the header Join Akines button, (2) the stage headline should be two lines, not four, and (3) the stage animation should pause when the mouse enters or hovers on the section, with each state lasting 12 seconds before changing to the next.
- Header Join: it used a separate, simpler hover (the shared sweep pseudo-elements were switched off for it in base.css). Now it uses the shared `.btn` system: rising fill (`::before`, 0.55 s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)), colour swap and press-down (scale 0.97). The fill follows the header text colour via `--sweep: var(--hdr-fg)` in src/styles/header.css: white fill with ink text over the hero (`data-state='clear'`), ink fill with ivory text on the solid header (`data-state='solid'`). The `display: none` rule for `.btn--header::before, ::after` was removed from base.css. Hover colours are inside `@media (hover: hover)`.
- Two-line headline: root cause was a fixed `max-width: 821px` on `.stage__headline` (left over from a narrower layout) plus type that was too large at tablet width, so each of the two intended lines wrapped again. Fix in src/styles/trust.css (768 and up): `.stage__copy { container-type: inline-size }` and `.stage__headline { max-width: none; font-size: min(var(--fs-stage), calc(100cqw / 27.6)); }`, so the type scales with the column (about 24 px at 768, 28 at 1194, 32 from 1280). The explicit `\n` line breaks in content.ts still control where the two lines break. Mobile (under 768) is unchanged and still wraps naturally.
- Timing: `trust.autoAdvanceSeconds` in src/content/content.ts changed from 6 to 12. The loader inside the active chip runs for exactly that long (CSS animation, `animationend` advances the stage).
- Pause: the pause on hover already existed (`.stage:hover .chip__loader { animation-play-state: paused }` inside `@media (hover: hover)`, plus focus and the pause button). It was verified to cover the whole stage (left edge, centre, right edge, on a chip), not only the chips, so no code change was needed. It resumes from the same point when the mouse leaves. Optional idea not built: pause or restart the carousel while the stage is off screen.
- Verification: nf/test2.js, 29 of 29 on both builds (Join at rest, hover mid-animation, hover end, press, return to rest, both header states; headlines two lines at 768, 820, 1024, 1194, 1280, 1440, 1512, 1920 and 1999 px; mobile keeps natural wrapping; 12 s duration; pause at four positions; resume; advance at 12 s with a fresh 12 s run; no advance at 11.9 s while hovered; reduced motion has no loader). Regressions on the Next.js build: 52 of 52 functional (the 12 s checks seek the loader animation instead of waiting), 23 of 23 motion, 9 of 9 stagger, 41 of 41 for the 404 page. Preview artifact: 29 of 29 for this request, 23 of 23 for the 404 view, 56 of 56 functional, 22 of 23 motion (the hash check differs by design), 9 of 9 stagger.
- Follow-up the same morning (05:25 Lagos): the user then asked to remove the pause on hover from this section and keep the 12 seconds per state. Removed the `@media (hover: hover) { .stage:hover .chip__loader { animation-play-state: paused } }` rule from src/styles/trust.css and updated the comment in Trust.tsx. Kept on purpose (not hover related, accessibility): the loader still pauses for keyboard focus inside the stage (`.stage:has(:focus-visible)`) and with the visible-on-focus pause button (`.stage.is-paused`). The earlier bullet about hover pause describes what was built first; the current behaviour is no pause on hover. Tests changed accordingly: hovering at four positions keeps the loader running, the stage still advances at 12 s with the pointer on it, a fresh 12 s run starts, and the pause button still pauses and resumes (after releasing focus, because focus alone also pauses). Results: header/trust suite 28 of 28 on both builds, functional 52 of 52 (Next) and 56 of 56 (preview), motion 23 of 23 (Next) and 22 of 23 (preview, hash check by design), stagger 9 of 9, 404 41 of 41 (Next) and 23 of 23 (preview).
- Tooling note for whoever re-runs the tests: after a rebuild the Next image optimizer can hang on some images, which makes Playwright's `networkidle` wait time out. Restarting `next start` fixes it.

### Header hides on scroll down and returns on scroll up, added 29 Oct 2026 (05:45 Lagos)
The user asked: when scrolling down the header should hide by animating up the screen, then appear back when scrolling up by animating down, and remain fixed to the top when scrolling up.
- Behaviour: scrolling down slides the header up out of view (translate to -100% minus 2 px and the safe area, 0.45 s, `--ease-io`); scrolling up slides it back down (0.6 s, `--ease-out`, soft landing) and it stays fixed at the top while scrolling up. It needs 8 px of travel in one direction to react (`TRAVEL` in Header.tsx), so trackpad wobble does nothing. The scroll position is clamped to the real scroll range so rubber-banding at the top or bottom is not read as a direction.
- Always visible: within the first header height from the top (so over the hero it stays and keeps its clear then solid states), while the menu is open, when keyboard focus enters the header (`onFocus`, so tabbing or shift-tabbing back to it reveals it), and while a link glides to a section.
- Decision I took without asking: menu and in-section links (anything that calls `scrollToId`) are treated as navigation, not scrolling. `scrollToId` in src/lib/motion.ts sets `data-gliding` on the html element during the glide (and for 150 ms after, or around an instant jump), the header stays visible, and the section lands under it at the anchor offset with no empty gap. The next scroll down hides it. If the user prefers the header to hide during link glides too, remove the `data-gliding` check in Header.tsx (and app.js in the preview), but sections will then land with a 64 to 80 px empty band above them.
- Reduced motion: still hides and shows, but at once (the global reduced-motion rule sets transition duration to about 0), no slide.
- Files: src/components/Header.tsx (state `hidden`, `data-hidden` attribute, refs for travel and last position, `bar` ref), src/styles/header.css (`translate` transition and the `[data-hidden='true']` rule), src/lib/motion.ts (`data-gliding`). The 404 page has its own non-fixed header and is unaffected. In the preview, the same logic is plain JavaScript in app.js (`updateHeader`, `setHeaderHidden`, `focusin`), and the safe-area term in the translate keeps the hidden header off the phone status-bar band.
- Verification: new script nf/test3.js, 52 of 52 on both builds (desktop 1512, tablet 1194 and mobile 402: visible and clear at the top, stays while within its own height, slides up on scroll down, stays hidden further down, a 4 px wobble does not show it, slides down animated with no bounce (sampled positions -68, -31, -14, -6, -3, -1, 0), fixed at the top while scrolling up, hides again, returns solid below the hero, clear again at the top, no console messages; real mouse wheel; keyboard PageDown, PageUp, Home and focus reveal; menu open keeps it visible, a menu link glides with it visible and lands at 80 px with no gap, then the next scroll down hides it; reduced motion instant). One older check was adjusted: nf/test2.js now scrolls up 40 px before hovering the Join button on the solid header, because the header is hidden after scrolling down. Regressions: 52 of 52 functional, 23 of 23 motion, 9 of 9 stagger, 41 of 41 404, 28 of 28 header and trust on the Next build; on the preview 56 of 56, 22 of 23 (hash check by design), 9 of 9, 23 of 23 and 28 of 28.
- Not built (not requested): revealing the header when the mouse moves to the top edge of the window.

### Full-width photo sections on wide screens, added 29 Oct 2026 (08:04 Lagos)
The user sent two screenshots (the trust stage, and the closing photo of five people walking) that did not fill the width of their desktop, and asked for them to fill the full width of any screen size. In the screenshots the stage was 1694 px wide and the photo 1701 px wide, left-aligned, with an empty ivory strip on the right (the black strip on the left is outside the page).
- Cause: `aspect-ratio` plus `max-height` on a block with automatic width. Once the height cap is reached the browser narrows the width to keep the ratio instead of cropping, so the box stops growing (stage 1100 x 1512/982 = 1694; closing photo 900 x 1.89 = 1701) and sits at the left. It began at about 1700 px wide for those two.
- Same defect, fixed in the same change: `.immersive` (philosophy photo section, cap 1080 so it began at about 1900 px) and `.fullcard` (the experience cards, same cap). The hero was never affected (no aspect ratio). The community photo is not full-bleed by design (inset 64 px inside the 1512 container) and was left alone.
- Fix: `width: 100%` added next to the aspect ratio in the desktop rules of src/styles/trust.css (`.stage`), closing.css (`.closing__photo`), rhythm.css (`.immersive`) and experiences.css (`.fullcard`), each with a comment. Beyond the cap the photo is cropped by `object-fit: cover` instead (checked visually at 1920 x 1080 and 2560 x 1300: the faces and the copy stay in frame, the section content stays centred in the 1512 container). Page height is unchanged at 402, 1194, 1280 and 1512 (measured with and without the rule).
- Verification: new script nf/test4.js, 50 of 50 on both builds (the four sections span the full width at 402, 768, 1194, 1280, 1512, 1728, 1920, 2200, 2560 and 3440 px; no horizontal overflow). Regressions on the Next build: 52 of 52 functional, 23 of 23 motion, 9 of 9 stagger, 41 of 41 404, 28 of 28 header and trust, 52 of 52 header hide on scroll; on the preview 56 of 56, 22 of 23 (hash check by design), 9 of 9, 23 of 23, 28 of 28, 52 of 52.
- Tooling note: when restarting the local test server, kill the old next-server by its real pid (find it by scanning /proc, the pid file only holds the shell wrapper); an old server left running over a rebuilt .next serves pages without their CSS.

### Mobile "How it works" step card with swiping text, added 29 Oct 2026 (08:27 Lagos)
The user attached Rhythm_Steps.pdf (the mobile design of the stepper) and asked for the section to look exactly like it on mobile only, with the same premium animations, but with the text swiping smoothly from side to side depending on which navigation button is tapped. Tablet and desktop keep the existing stepper unchanged.
- Design as built (mobile, under 768 px, 402 design width): the photo is 354 x 350 (`aspect-ratio: 354 / 350` on `.rhythm__stage`), then the card: a 38 px round icon badge with a 1 px `#dadada` border (new token `--border-card`) beside the serif 24 px step title, the one-line summary (16 px ink), the description (16 px muted), then the navigation row: outlined 48 px round previous and next buttons around the counter "01 / 05" (serif 24 px, the current number in ink, the slash and total in `#c0c0c0`), then a full-width "Explore the Akines philosophy" pill with the arrow. The old two-button controls and the numbered row list are hidden on mobile (`display: none`); tablet and desktop hide the new card.
- Measured against the PDF: section height 819.89 px against 819.9, every element within about 1 px, the round arrow ink matches; the CTA arrow ink measured 117 against 127 in the design, accepted.
- Swipe: all five slides stay in the DOM (good for search, no JavaScript and screen readers). The active slide is in normal flow so the section has the right height before any script runs; the others are absolutely positioned and hidden. Tapping next sends the old text out to the left (38% of the card width, fading in 0.3 s) and brings the new text in from the right (55% of the width over 1 s, `--ease-out`); tapping previous mirrors it. The title row, line and description cascade 55 ms apart (`--ci` 0, 1, 2), the old text leaves 40 ms apart, and the counter number rises in. The card height eases (0.6 s) between slides because step 5 is taller (slide heights 165, 165, 165, 165 and 191 px), so the buttons and the button below glide instead of jumping. The photo still crossfades, as before. Next from 05 wraps to 01 and previous from 01 wraps to 05, with the swipe direction following the button tapped (not the wrap).
- Nothing animates on load: `data-moved` is only set on the first tap, so the text does not slide in when the page loads. Rapid taps are handled (the leaving slide is cleared after 1 s, only the latest swipe is shown). The card itself reveals on scroll like the rest of the page (`.card__viewport`, `.card__nav` in the UP list in motion.ts).
- Reduced motion: no slide, no fade, no height easing; the text changes at once. Accessibility: each slide is a labelled group (`aria-roledescription="slide"`), inactive slides are `aria-hidden`, the counter has a polite live region ("Step 02 of 05"), the buttons are named Previous step and Next step.
- Not built (not requested): touch swipe gestures on the card itself. Only the two buttons move it, as the user described. It would be a small addition if wanted.
- Files: src/components/Rhythm.tsx (`useCardHeight`, `swipe`, the card markup), src/styles/rhythm.css (card, badge, nav, outlined round buttons, mobile overrides), src/styles/motion.css (the swipe keyframes, `--dir`, reduced motion rule), src/styles/tokens.css (`--border-card`), src/lib/motion.ts (card elements added to the reveal list). The preview page has the same logic as plain JavaScript in app.js (`setCard`, `syncCardHeight`, called from `setStep`).
- Verification: new script nf/test5.js, 37 of 37 on both builds (layout against the PDF, the counter, both directions, wrap-around, the old text leaving while the new text arrives, height easing, no animation on load, rapid taps, reduced motion, hidden on tablet and desktop, no overflow, no console messages). Regressions on the Next build: 52 of 52 functional, 23 of 23 motion, 9 of 9 stagger, 41 of 41 404, 28 of 28 header and trust, 52 of 52 header hide, 50 of 50 full width; on the preview 56 of 56, 22 of 23 (hash check by design), 9 of 9, 23 of 23, 28 of 28, 52 of 52, 50 of 50. The reveal checks in the motion script (art/motiontest.js) that counted hidden elements were changed to ignore elements that are not rendered (the card is display none on tablet and desktop, and the numbered list is display none on mobile); a new script nf/resize.js (2 of 2 on both builds) confirms the card is still revealed after a window is resized from wide to narrow, and the list after narrow to wide.
- Tooling note: the Next image optimizer can hang after a rebuild and make Playwright wait for the network to go idle; restart `next start` if a test stalls on load.

Every route was visited at 1512, 1194 and 402 (scripts/check-routes.mjs): no console errors, no horizontal overflow, no broken images; the same 132 routes were visited on the Next production server at 1512 and 402 (only /404 returns 404, as intended). Sign-in from the landing page and the whole join flow were walked end to end at three sizes (both end on /community).

(changed from Akines2026! on 3 Nov so the strength meter reads Strong), confirmation code 123456, name Kemi Adeyemi, initials KA. In the join flow the registered values are ndegoyit@example.com and 0803 123 4567 (Figma frame 1e), a wrong code is any six digits except 000000 (which plays the expired state). Session key in localStorage: akines.session.v1 ('1' = signed in). Signed-out visitors who open a signed-in route are sent to the sign-in panel and returned afterwards.
- Architecture: one React source compiled twice. (a) Next.js 15 App Router: landing at src/app/page.tsx, every other screen through the required catch-all src/app/[...slug]/page.tsx with generateStaticParams from the route registry (src/routes.ts, one routes file per feature under src/features). (b) The preview artifact: scripts/build-preview.mjs bundles src/artifact/main.tsx with esbuild into one HTML fragment (fonts inlined), swapping src/lib/nav.tsx for the hash router src/lib/nav.hash.tsx, src/lib/assets.ts for assets.preview.ts and next/image for a shim. Navigation always goes through @/lib/nav (Link, useNav, usePath). Rule learned the hard way: a module marked 'use client' cannot export data the route registry reads on the server, so route data lives in plain .ts files (join-frames.ts, signin-frames.ts) and only components live in client files.
- Shell: src/app-shell (signed-in header option A at desktop, tablet header, mobile header with unread dot, bottom bar and page sheets, profile sheet, avatar menu, tooltips, counters Messages 3 and Notifications 12), routes shell types site, app and bare, with the soft sign-in guard. UI kit (src/ui, KIT_API.md): Button, IconButton, Chip, Tag, Avatar, Field, TextInput, TextArea, Select, Checkbox, Radio, Toggle, Tabs, Modal, Drawer, Menu, Tooltip, Skeleton, EmptyState, InlineError and toast.show(). Toasts sit bottom left (x 64, 48 above the foot) on desktop as in the Figma Toast frames, 2.5 s, centred above the bottom bar on tablet and mobile. Modal scrim: flat #181818 at 28% on Community screens, blurred black at 35% elsewhere (set through html[data-page]).
- Motion: the landing language reused (src/lib/motion.ts and motion.css generalised with data-m="up | stagger | words | frame", a re-runnable scan with a MutationObserver so screens that render later still reveal); route change fades and rises; buttons use the rising sweep; overlays rise 0.45 s, drawers slide 0.55 s, sheets 0.6 s, exits 0.28 s; reduced motion shows everything at once.
- Landing and menu: re-wired to the Figma prototype. Header and footer Sign in open the slide-out panel in place; Join Akines and Join the community go to /join; hero and closing "Find my kind of connection" open the panel; menu Sign in and Join work at three sizes; /sign-in is also a real route (landing with the panel open).

Tools in the project: tools/figdump.js and tools/listframes.js (read-only Figma scripts), scripts/img.mjs (photo converter), scripts/build-preview.mjs, scripts/check-routes.mjs (visits every route at three widths).
- To refresh the artifact after code changes: `node scripts/build-preview.mjs <folder under the session scratchpad>` (it writes artifact.html and images/), then publish artifact.html to the same URL with `root` set to that folder and every file under images/ in `files`. Images in public/images/app/<feature>/ are copied automatically (62 files, 6.4 MB).
### Preview artifact (Claude), added 28 Oct 2026
The user asked for a Claude Artifact to preview the coded page. Published as "Akines Landing Page": https://claude.ai/artifact/RjyEARNFhguXmvyzipTA1L (republished on 29 Oct with the motion layer, again with the 404 page, and again with the header hover, two-line headline and 12 second timing, and once more with the hover pause removed, again with the hide-on-scroll header, again with the full-width fix, and once more with the mobile swipe card, version 8; at publish time its sharing was set to anyone with the link, which can be changed from the artifact's Share menu).
- How it was made: the page markup is the production build's server-rendered HTML (same classes and DOM), the CSS is the project's own src/styles files in layout order, and the 25 images from public/images are published beside the page and referenced as images/<name>.jpg. Alice and Manrope load from Google Fonts (the artifact sandbox only allows that font host).
- Behaviour: Next.js and React are not used in the artifact (its sandbox blocks the app's chunks). Header state, menu panel/modal (with the closing animation), stepper (with the reserved list height and the mobile swipe card) and trust carousel are plain JavaScript ported line by line from Header, Menu, Rhythm and Trust. The motion layer is the same code as the Next.js build: motion.css is part of the inlined CSS, motion.ts is transpiled with TypeScript and embedded, and the same head script runs first. In-page links glide to the section without changing the address bar (the artifact page only allows plain #name hashes). (Links to the live app no longer exist: every CTA now opens the 404 view, see above.)
- Differences from the Next.js build: no next/image optimization (full JPEGs, 5 MB total, lazy loaded), a single light look (no dark theme), and two small additions for phone apps (fixed header and mobile menu clear the status bar). Copy, layout and behaviour are otherwise the same.
- Verification: the exported file was served locally and passed the same functional script as the Next.js build (menu on three sizes, scroll links, stepper, carousel, reduced motion, no overflow 402 to 1920, no console errors, every visible image loads, no hash changes), plus the motion and stagger scripts (22 of 23 motion checks pass; the one difference is the hash check, which expects the Next.js build to update the address bar and the artifact deliberately does not). Full-page screenshots at 1512, 1194 and 402 have identical page heights to the Next.js build with reduced motion; after blurring out image compression the largest pixel difference is 10 of 255.
- To refresh it after code changes: rebuild the project, re-export the rendered HTML, rerun the assembler (it lives in the session scratchpad, not in the zip) and publish the same file to the same URL.


## Goal
Redesign the Akines landing page (current live page: https://akines.vercel.app/about) in Figma, desktop only at 1512 px first. Look and feel: Dior-style editorial luxury (full-bleed campaign imagery, restrained navigation, serif headlines, generous whitespace), applied to Akines context and imagery. Do not copy Dior's assets, logo, copy or a one-to-one layout. Tablet and mobile come after the desktop design is approved.

## Source documents (provided by the user)
- "Editorial Landing Page Blueprint": section-by-section copy, layout and image direction.
- "Landing Page Redesign PRD": requirements, acceptance criteria, analytics events, delivery plan.
Use the copy in those documents as written.

- Figma session id used by Claude this round: ef24ba76-1faf-4939-b91a-09ee4903a889 (skillNames "resource:figma-use,resource:figma-generate-design")

## Design system found in the file (use it, do not add new tokens)
- Variable collections: Colour Primitives, Typography Primitives, Typography Tokens, Colour Semantics (Light and Dark).
- Fonts: Alice (serif headlines), Manrope (body and buttons).
- Text styles used so far: Headings/Heading (32, hero headline and panel headings), Titles/Subtitle (48, section 02 headline), Button/Body/Large (18, intro copy), Button/Body/Base (16, panel body), Button/Body/Small (14, hero eyebrow and nav), Button/Body/Small Bold (14, links and buttons), Button/Body/Caption (12, uppercase labels with 14% letter spacing). Titles/Display 1 (96) and Display 2 (72) are available.
- Colour variable ids (Colour Semantics): Text/Primary 10011:102, Text/Secondary 10011:103, Text/Inverse 10011:107, Icon/Primary 10011:117, Icon/Inverse 10011:121, Surface/Akines/Primary/Strong 10011:1128 (ivory #F4F1EB), Surface/Akines/Primary/Subtle 10011:1127, Border/Subtle 10011:1136, Action/Akines/Secondary/Default 10013:8 (amber #F4A63A). Icon/Secondary exists (look up by name).
- Spacing and radius variables are in a remote library, so spacing is hard-coded (8, 16, 20, 28, 48, 64, 96, 112, 144).
- Logo is cloned from the current nav bar (16018:1621).

## Decisions
- Tokens: follow the file's identity (ivory #F4F1EB, near-black #181818, amber #F4A63A, Alice plus Manrope) instead of the blueprint's unverified values (orange #E56B3F, Playfair or Lora). User did not object.
- Buttons are fully rounded (corner radius 999) because Akines is a social and community product. Applies to the header Join Akines and both hero CTAs.
- Images are square-cornered (radius 0) for the editorial look.
- Hero: menu icon left, logo centre, Sign in plus outlined Join Akines pill right. Description paragraph removed, small centred serif headline (32 px), eyebrow in plain sentence case, no accent rule. User approved the hero.
- Hero CTAs kept (PRD requires a clear next step): primary ivory pill "Find my kind of connection", secondary outlined pill "Discover Akines". User may ask to drop the secondary.
- Section 02: copy taken from the blueprint as written. Eyebrow is an uppercase 12 px caption with no accent rule (matches the hero's restraint). Headline is hard-broken into two even lines ("Every connection begins / with a reason.") to avoid a stranded word. Panels are staggered in two columns with portrait and landscape images alternating, one image, label with icon, heading, short paragraph and one underlined discovery link per panel. Hover zoom on images is an annotation for development only (not drawn).
- Imagery: the user supplied the hero photo. Section images are generated inside Figma with model gemini-3.1-flash-image (uses Figma AI credits; 5 calls so far for section 02 because the fourth image had to be regenerated after it came back with a black frame). Generated image URLs expire after 7 days; the images live in the file as fills. Prompts use candid editorial direction, diverse people, warm natural light, 35mm film look, and end with "No text, no logos, no watermarks". For future prompts add "no border, no frame, no signage" since one image came back framed and with a sign.
- Section 03 interactivity (added at the user's request): the five step states were converted into one component set so the stepper can be played in a prototype. Transitions use smart animate so the active row expands and the photo crossfades; fall back to DISSOLVE if smart animate looks wrong on a row swap. No scroll lock, content stays readable without animation. Dev note: respect reduced motion by disabling the crossfade.
- Section 03 layout: full-bleed immersive photo first (as the blueprint asks), with the headline "Meaningful connection / doesn't happen all at once." and supporting copy overlaid on a bottom scrim (measured median contrast of white text over the photo: eyebrow 8.1:1, headline 13.3:1, body copy 16.4:1). Below, a five-step stage: one large step image with round previous and next controls, and a list where the active step is expanded and the other four stay compact with thumbnails. This follows the blueprint rule of not showing five large cards at once, keeps all five steps visible with large numbers, supports direct selection and crossfade (annotation only, not drawn), and never locks scrolling. The separate step-states section shows the layout with each other step active so all five descriptions and images can be reviewed.
- Step images generated for section 03: arrive (woman at a warm doorway), understand (close portrait listening), do (friends playing a board game), reflect (man with notebook by a window), continue (two friends on riverside steps), plus the immersive communal table photo. Total Figma AI credits used so far: 28 generate_image calls (22 before section 05, 4 for section 05, 1 for section 06, 1 for section 07, no retries needed) (5 for section 02 including one regeneration, 6 for section 03, 6 for the first section 04 version, 5 for the chosen section 04 arrangement including one regeneration).
- Tooling note: generate_image replaced the fill in place only for plain rectangles on the current page that were not inside an auto-layout frame inside a section. For the five source slots (inside an auto-layout frame inside a section) it created five new "Generated Image" frames on the first page ("Title Page") instead. Their fills were copied into the slots and the stray frames were deleted. Next time, generate into standalone rectangles (like the immersive image) or check the returned nodeId matches the requested one.
- Generated images are placeholders for real Akines photography; recommend replacing before launch.
- Tooling notes from section 05: (1) generate_image created new "Generated Image" frames on the first page ("Title Page") even for top-level frames made in the same session (returned nodeIds were 16844:149 to 16847:149, not the requested ones). The fills were copied to the source frames with node.fills = stray.fills and the strays deleted. Always check the returned nodeId. (2) figma.createAutoLayout frames come with a white fill; set fills = [] on layout-only frames. (3) Paint opacity on variable-bound paints did not survive on instances (rendered fully opaque); use layer opacity or separate layers instead. (4) Reaction easing names are EASE_IN, EASE_OUT, EASE_IN_AND_OUT, LINEAR and so on (not EASE_IN_OUT); trigger AFTER_TIMEOUT works on component variants; variant reactions carry to instances. (5) A large validation error from setReactionsAsync is usually one bad enum, so truncate the message. (6) Section 06: generate_image again created a new frame on the first page (16851:149); its fill was copied to the image rect and the frame deleted. Raw generated images can carry thin dark bands at the edges; crop with a fill of scaleMode CROP and imageTransform [[0.89,0,0.055],[0,0.94,0.03]] (visible portion, then offset). A text node resized after cloning may keep a stale height; call resize then set textAutoResize = HEIGHT again. (7) Section 07: generate_image again created a new frame (16855:149); fill copied and frame deleted. Buttons cloned from the hero need their height set explicitly (counterAxisSizingMode FIXED then resize) if two different clones must match.
- Section 04 layout (user chosen): two full-screen image cards (KinCircle, My Store) alternate with two two-column rows (Banter small and Create big, then Play Together big and Wellbeing small), so the page never reads as a uniform card grid. The small column in each two-column row is dropped 120 px. Full-screen cards use overlay text (icon and label, serif name, tagline, description, underlined link) with text bottom left, then bottom right. The two-column stories use the Story Caption component below the image. All copy is as written in the blueprint.
- Section 04 governance: the blueprint and PRD say unimplemented features must not be shown as live and concept previews must be labelled. Story Caption has a "Concept preview" boolean for this; it is off everywhere because it is not known which experiences are live. The user should confirm which of KinCircle, Banter, Create, Play Together, My Store and Wellbeing are live, and every CTA needs a real destination (or an explanatory section) before launch.
- Section 04 order: KinCircle, Banter, Create, My Store, Play Together, Wellbeing. This swaps My Store and Play Together compared with the blueprint order (the user asked for it). Visual order matches DOM order, so mobile can stack in this same order.
- Section 04 images: KinCircle wide, Create landscape, My Store wide (regenerated once because the first came back as a split collage with a visible seam; use "one single continuous photograph, no collage, no seams" in prompts) and Wellbeing portrait (a young man). Banter and Play Together keep the photos from the first version.
- Section 05 decisions (28 Oct 2026): the user supplied a PDF mock-up and their own reference frame. One full-bleed photo card with a centred 2-line headline and four pill chips along the bottom; the first chip carries a translucent loader that fills over 6 seconds, then the next chip activates and image, eyebrow and headline change together; loops after the fourth; chips have line icons (git-compare-arrows, shield-check, heart, sprout, as suggested by the Blueprint). The card is 1512 x 982 to match the user's reference frame and the hero (Claude had said 900 in the confirmation message). The Blueprint's section eyebrow, headline, supporting copy and CTA are in an intro block above the card so the card stays as the user drew it. Chip labels are SemiBold 14 in every state; inactive chips are dimmed with opacity.
- Section 05 governance: copy follows the Blueprint; no compatibility, safety or verification guarantees. The wellbeing headline is shortened and drops the professional-care disclaimer from the card (to live on the policy page). The CTA needs a real destination (privacy, safety and community policies). Measured contrast on all four photos (text colour against the darkened photo, excluding text pixels): eyebrow 11.9 to 14.6:1 median, 6.0 to 8.0:1 worst case; headline 17.3 to 18.6:1 median, 10.9 to 11.5:1 worst case; chip labels 6.7 to 14.7:1 median, 5.8:1 worst case. All pass 4.5:1.
- Section 06 decisions (28 Oct 2026): the user said all sections were approved and to design the next one. Layout deliberately differs from the left and right intros above it: centred intro, one wide inset photo, a three-up row that spreads the Blueprint sentence ("A conversation can become a friendship. A shared interest can become a community. An idea can become something that people build together.") across icons, then a quiet lighter panel with the early-stage invitation as the main message. Chosen because the PRD says fallback content must stay useful before a community exists and forbids invented stories, statistics and testimonials. Suggested icons quote, users-round and trending-up are used as the Blueprint suggests. The photo is the only imagery (1 generate_image call; total now 27).
- Section 07 decisions (28 Oct 2026): closing statement set at 60 px (larger than the 48 px section headlines) so it reads as the finale; centred, only two actions, primary is the filled dark pill and is visually dominant, secondary is outlined; buttons made 62 px high (the hero secondary CTA is 2 px taller than its primary by inheritance; fixed here only). The text sits on ivory below the image rather than over it, which separates it from the overlay text used in sections 03 to 05. The headline break moved to "...looking for / may be looking for..." to avoid splitting the phrase "may be looking". 1 generate_image call (total now 28).
- Footer decisions (28 Oct 2026): quiet, functional close on the same ivory as the page, no image and no campaign copy, as the Blueprint asks. Column and legal copy is as written in the Blueprint (Discover, About Akines, Your trust, Your account, Legal strip). The wordmark is the header logo (renders AKINES). Footer has no CTA button; Join Akines and Sign in are plain links in Your account. Every link needs a real destination (annotation only in the design). Contrast measured on the render: links 8.4:1, legal links 10:1. Text/Secondary (#6F6F6F) on the ivory is 4.46:1, just under AA 4.5:1 for small text; this affects the 12 px uppercase eyebrows (section eyebrows and footer column headings), so the token or the eyebrow colour needs a decision from the user (not changed, no tokens added). The 24 px tagline is large text and passes. The copyright line was set to Text/Primary for that reason.
- Scrolled header decisions (28 Oct 2026): sticky, appears after the page scrolls past the 96 px over-hero header; 80 px high (16 px shorter); solid ivory (Surface/Akines/Primary/Strong), 1 px Border/Subtle bottom edge only (strokeBottomWeight 1, other sides 0), no shadow; logo text, menu icon strokes (Icon/Primary), Sign in and the Join Akines outline and label all switch to the dark text colour; Join Akines stays an outlined pill (a filled dark pill is the alternative if a stronger action is wanted). Transition 200 ms ease-out, instant with reduced motion. Tooling note: cloning an absolutely positioned header and moving it into an auto-layout frame keeps ABSOLUTE positioning, so set layoutPositioning = 'AUTO' before layoutSizingHorizontal = 'FILL'.
- Scroll prototype decisions (28 Oct 2026): the user proposed the method (duplicate the whole landing page, one with the transparent header and one with the solid header, both headers fixed, switch on mouse enter between the hero and the next section) and asked that both copies keep the page's natural hugging height instead of a fixed 982 px viewport. Figma has no scroll-position trigger, so the pointer position decides the switch; the 96 px threshold stays in the annotation for developers. Tooling notes: (8) this API has no scrollBehavior property; fixed position is set with frame.numberOfFixedChildren (the last n children are fixed, so the header must be the last child). A header cloned from an absolute child keeps layoutPositioning ABSOLUTE, so set layoutSizingHorizontal to FIXED before making it absolute. (9) Cloning a frame that is a flow starting point copies the flow entry, so duplicate entries appear; filter them and rename (setting a list with duplicate nodeIds errors). (10) A use_figma call that throws appears to roll back its changes, so a failed run leaves nothing half-built (seen once; still check). (11) Detaching a stage instance drops its inherited prototype reactions.
- Tablet decisions (28 Oct 2026): the user asked for tablet landscape at 1194 px, design only, on the existing Tablet page, as a scaled-down version of the desktop that keeps the two-column layouts. Method: the Desktop wrapper was cloned onto the Tablet page and frame.rescale(1194/1512 = 0.7897) applied, so proportions, image crops and column splits match desktop exactly (hero 775 and full-screen cards 679 high are the scaled heights, not 834). Then: (a) type remapped to a readable tablet scale: 72 to 56 (step numbers), 60 to 48 (closing headline), 48 to 40 (section headlines), 48 to 36 for the Early Community Invitation headline only so it holds two lines, 32 to 28, 24 to 22, 20 to 18, 18 to 16, 16 stays 16, 14 stays 14, 12 stays 12, logo wordmark stays scaled at 15.8; (b) paddings and gaps snapped to a 4 or 8 grid (side padding 48, section padding 112, column gap 36 to 40); (c) strokes minimum 1 px, divider rectangles 1 px; (d) icons snapped to whole sizes (14, 16, 18 or 20) with a 1.5 stroke; (e) buttons 48 high, header Join Akines 44, Trust chips 44 (touch targets); (f) footer brand block 336 wide and link columns 150 wide with 40 gaps so 'Community guidelines' is not clipped and the tagline stays on one line; (g) Scrim gradient on the KinCircle and My Store cards strengthened (stops 0:0, 0.22:0, 0.5:0.5, 0.72:0.68, 1:0.86) because larger text sits higher on the photo. No new images and no new tokens. All instances in the tablet copy were detached (no component linkage on the Tablet page); type sizes are local overrides, so a proper fix is Tablet text styles or a Tablet mode in Typography Tokens (suggest to the user). Contrast over photos measured by hiding the text and sampling the photo behind each text box (white text against the 90th percentile brightness, worst case): hero eyebrow 8.9:1, hero headline 9.3:1, section 03 text 8.5 to 12.3:1, KinCircle 5.6 to 11.4:1, My Store 7.6 to 12.1:1, section 05 eyebrow 13.2:1, headline 11.9:1, chip labels 8.3 to 11.0:1. All pass 4.5:1 for small text and 3:1 for large. Still open from desktop: Text/Secondary on ivory is 4.46:1 for the 12 px eyebrows. Tooling notes: (12) frame.rescale works on a whole frame including instances, but text inside instances keeps the scaled size and fontSize assignments inside instances are silently ignored, so detach instances (outermost first, repeat until none remain) before changing type. (13) Cloning the flow-start wrapper on the Desktop page collapsed the page's flowStartingPoints from 5 entries to 3; it was restored (01 Landing, 02 Authentication, 03 Editorial Landing, Landing (header switches on hover), Matching and trust auto-advance). After cloning a flow-start frame always re-read flowStartingPoints. (14) A frame's text can be hidden with visible = false, screenshotted and measured in Python, then restored; always restore and confirm no hidden texts remain.
- Mobile decisions (28 Oct 2026): the user asked for mobile at 402 px (not 390) on the Mobile page, design only. Method: the tablet wrapper (no instances left) was cloned onto the Mobile page and restructured section by section to one column with 24 px side padding (content width 354), so the copy, imagery and component look match the approved desktop and tablet. Type scale: 56 to 40 (step numerals), 48 to 32, 40 to 28 (section headlines), 36 to 28, 28 to 24, 22 to 20; 32 section titles on the full-screen cards, 28 for the hero headline, 22 for the section 05 stage headline; 16, 14 and 12 stay. Hard line breaks in headlines were replaced with spaces so text wraps naturally. Layout by section: Header over hero 64 high with menu left, logo centre, outlined Join Akines pill (108 x 44) right and Sign in hidden; Hero 402 x 798 (photo crop shifted to keep the faces, CTAs stacked and full width, 48 high); Section 02 panels stacked (portrait 440 high for 01 and 04, landscape 264 for 02 and 03, gap 56); Section 03 immersive card 402 x 640 with text at the bottom, then the step stage image (354 x 440) with the previous and next controls at its bottom right, five rows (number, title and line, 48 x 60 thumbnail) and a full width outlined CTA; Section 04 intro stacked, KinCircle and My Store as 402 x 640 cards, Banter, Create, Play Together and Wellbeing stacked in the same order as desktop; Section 05 intro stacked, stage 402 x 740 with the four chips stacked full width (44 high); Section 06 intro centred, wide photo 354 x 340, the three proof moments stacked with an icon beside each sentence and hairlines between, invitation panel stacked with a full width CTA; Section 07 photo 402 x 420 then text, stacked full width CTAs (48 high); Footer brand, then link columns in a 2 x 2 grid (165 wide columns, 24 gap, 36 row gap), then a left aligned legal strip. Scrims strengthened on the mobile hero (stops 0:0.62, 0.09:0.6, 0.18:0.3, 0.32:0, 0.5:0.1, 0.6:0.32, 0.74:0.52, 1:0.84), the section 03 immersive card (0:0, 0.3:0, 0.5:0.45, 0.66:0.66, 0.82:0.78, 1:0.88) and the section 05 stage (0:0, 0.25:0, 0.45:0.5, 0.6:0.64, 0.78:0.78, 1:0.9). Contrast over photos measured with the hide-text method (white text against the 90th percentile brightness): hero eyebrow 6.2:1, headline 9.4:1, header logo 6.3:1, Join Akines 6.3:1; section 03 eyebrow 6.3:1, headline 6.4:1, body 11.4 to 15.5:1; KinCircle 5.7 to 13.4:1; My Store 9.4 to 19.7:1; section 05 eyebrow 6.7:1, headline 6.7:1, chip labels 12.5 to 14.2:1. All pass 4.5:1. An overflow and clipping audit (text wider than the wrapper, fixed text boxes, fixed-height stacks whose content is taller, rows wider than their frame) reports no issues. No new images, no new tokens, all instances already detached (no component linkage on the Mobile page, type sizes are local overrides). Tooling notes: (15) a frame switched from HORIZONTAL to VERTICAL keeps its old primaryAxisAlignItems; SPACE_BETWEEN makes itemSpacing do nothing on a hugging frame, so set it to MIN (found on the Early Community Invitation, Footer Main and Legal). (16) A frame switched from horizontal to vertical keeps its stale fixed height; set layoutSizingVertical to HUG, otherwise the content overflows the frame and is cut off by the section clip (this hid a 780 px overflow in Intention Grid until the audit). (17) setPluginData is not supported in this runtime (use setSharedPluginData); a call that throws rolls back, so keep the list of node ids you hid and restore by id. (18) Read page.flowStartingPoints only after setCurrentPageAsync for that page; reading it for a page that is not loaded returned stale names. Desktop flows confirmed: 01 - Landing Page, 02 - Authentication, 03 - Editorial Landing Page, Landing (header switches on hover), Matching and trust (auto-advance) (the last one was re-added after being found missing).
- Menu decisions (28 Oct 2026): follows the reference screenshot (Close top left, plain list with chevrons, hairlines, content anchored to the top and a quiet block at the bottom, frosted panel over a dimmed and blurred page) with Akines styling: ivory glass instead of grey, Manrope links, the existing tagline and logo in place of the reference's bottom toggle. Only the four links the user named are shown; Sign in and Join Akines stay in the header on desktop and tablet and are the two actions on mobile. Chevrons are kept for fidelity to the reference, but they suggest sub-menus, so remove them if the links are plain section anchors. The user decided the links scroll to the sections on the same page (28 Oct, 'just make the links scroll to the sections'). The mobile Join Akines and Sign in buttons have no destination yet. Tooling notes: (19) overlayPositionType, overlayBackground and overlayBackgroundInteraction are read-only in this API; overlay frames default to CENTER with no built-in background, so each overlay frame is made the exact size of the viewport (1512 x 982, 1194 x 834, 402 x 874) and draws its own dim and blur; if the Play window is taller, change the position in the interaction panel. (20) A background blur effect on a frame renders in screenshots and in the prototype, so an overlay placed directly over a preview frame shows the real blurred look on the canvas. (21) A paint bound to a variable loses its opacity when set in the same call that creates it (it read back as 1); re-assign the paint with an explicit opacity afterwards (panel.fills = panel.fills.map(f => ({...f, opacity: 0.9}))), which kept the binding. (22) setReactionsAsync accepts the newer actions array ({type:'CLOSE'}, an OVERLAY node action, {type:'SET_VARIABLE', variableId, variableValue:{type:'BOOLEAN', resolvedType:'BOOLEAN', value}} and a SCROLL_TO node action); reading back also shows the legacy action field. (23) Scroll to cannot target the page from an overlay (Figma forum, Community Support: it only targets objects in the same top-level frame), so menus that must scroll are built as a layer inside the landing frame whose visibility is bound to a boolean prototype variable (node.setBoundVariable('visible', variable)); a fixed child needs numberOfFixedChildren and ABSOLUTE positioning.
- Decision log: on 28 Oct 2026 the user preferred Alt B. It was moved into the wrapper in place of the first version, which was deleted (its wide Create and Wellbeing photos are gone; credits were already spent).
- Typography: a typographic apostrophe is used in serif headlines (doesn’t). Section 02 body copy still uses straight apostrophes in Manrope (not changed because the section is approved).
- No em dashes in written work. Keep explanations short.

Design decisions:
- Desktop: a 1512 x 982 browser window (see "Screen format change" below): 88 px of Chrome on top, then a full-bleed 1512 x 894 split page with no margin, no sand canvas and no container radius. Form panel 544 wide (36 percent) on ivory (Surface/Akines/Primary/Strong, #F4F1EB) with 64 px side padding, photograph filling the other 968 px edge to edge. Form block is centred in the panel with the logo on top. (The first version had a sand canvas, Akines/Primary/600 #D2C5AD, with 72 / 60 padding around a 1392 x 838 rounded container; the user rejected that on 31 Oct.) Order follows the brief: heading, supporting line, Email address field, Continue, "or continue with" divider, Google, Apple, "Don't have an account? Create an account".
- Controls: 52 px pills. Field is white with a 1 px Icon/Secondary border (4.5:1). Continue is an ink pill (Action/Neutral). Provider buttons are outlined ink pills so no provider colour competes with Continue.
- Focus: Border/Focus in this file is ivory and invisible on ivory, so focus is a 2 px ink border plus a soft warm halo. Error uses Text/Error (#D31900) with an icon and a message that wraps; the message never relies on colour alone.
- Photograph: reused from the landing page (the conversation photograph, image hash 2269e0ee...), with a soft bottom scrim only. No new imagery.
- Overlay: Grey 950 (#181818) at 56 percent with a 24 px background blur, 16 px radius, 440 wide on desktop (400 on tablet), bottom left, inset 40 (32 on tablet). Mark, "Good to have you back.", one supporting sentence. No buttons or icons. On mobile it becomes a chip (mark and headline only, radius 14, inset 12) so the banner stays 188 high and every control stays above the fold; the supporting sentence is dropped there on purpose.
- Tablet landscape keeps the split with a 440 form panel. Tablet portrait stacks photo (420 high, overlay inside) over the form panel with the logo aligned to the form column. Both tablet frames are full-bleed since 31 Oct (sand padding and container radius removed, container renamed "Page"); the portrait form panel has 197 px side padding so the form column stays 440 wide. Mobile is one column: logo, banner, heading, supporting text, field, Continue, providers, sign-up link. Since 31 Oct (09:47) the mobile frames also sit under a 106 px browser bar, so the content area is 768 high and the banner is 136 high (was 188); the last line ends near y 800 of 874 (about 850 with a wrapped error message).
- Email first: no password field. The Success frame shows the email accepted (verified field, "Use a different email" link) and a dashed Next Step Slot annotation that stands in for password, one-time code or check-your-inbox. It is a placeholder, not a committed flow; remove it once the real step is designed.
- Meta exists only as an optional variant in the Social Button set, because the brief allows it only if the existing authentication supports it. It is not used in any screen.
- Provider marks keep their own colours. Check them against each provider's brand rules at build time; swap in the official assets if the team has them.

Limits and known quirks:
- The reference screenshot is not embedded. The workspace network allowlist blocks www.figma.com and mcp.figma.com, so images cannot be uploaded; the layout is redrawn in the Sign-in Reference section (A) on the Desktop page instead. Paste the original next to it if it should be archived in the file.
- Tooling notes: (24) A paint bound to a variable returns at opacity 1, and instances do not inherit paint-level opacity of bound fills, so the glass layer uses an unbound #181818 fill at 0.56 plus a BACKGROUND_BLUR effect on an absolute rectangle (node opacity would weaken the blur). (25) Nested instance text is set with setProperties on the nested instance; a wrapping error message needs the Message instance set to FILL width inside each Email Input variant and the Form Error text set to FILL with an icon slot, otherwise long messages are clipped. (26) A component set cannot hold loose layers, so the display-only dark backdrops behind the Light spinner and Light logo variants are absolute rectangles in the section frame, behind the set. (27) layoutSizingHorizontal/Vertical FILL or HUG must be set after the node is appended to an auto-layout parent; resize() resets sizing modes to FIXED. (28) combineAsVariants then set layoutWrap, itemSpacing and counterAxisSpacing on the set to lay the variants out. (29) Nodes can be moved between pages with newParent.appendChild(node) (page, section or frame); instances keep their links to components that moved to another page. Remove temporary pages only after they are empty and only while another page is current (page.remove()). (30) Section styling on this file's pages is a plain white fill with 400 px side and 300 px top and bottom padding around the frame (200 px side padding on Mobile); new sections copy the fill from a neighbouring section.
- Not yet checked with the user: whether Meta is supported, which methods are live (email link, password, one-time code), where "Create an account" leads, and which legal links belong on this screen. The state-to-state prototype links were added on 31 Oct (see below).
- No Figma AI credits were used for this work.

### Sign-in prototype, added 31 Oct 2026 (08:25 Lagos)
Request: link the sign in links and buttons, and every button that needs a signed-in user, to the sign-in screen, then prototype the sign-in screen interactions. Built in Figma only. It was not played from the workspace (no way to run Present mode here), so click through it once and report anything that feels off. Every reaction was read back and each destination checked as a top-level frame.

Links into the sign-in screen (click, navigate, dissolve 0.4 s):
- Desktop page, destination 1 Default (16956:149), 29 links: landing frame 16781:941 (header Sign in, Join Akines, Find my kind of connection twice, Join the Akines community, Explore Akines, footer Sign in and Join Akines), both header copies in the scroll prototype section 16863:2595, the scrolled header section 16861:1705 and the menu section 16890:2288.
- Tablet page, destination 16958:149, 11 links (landing 8, menu 3). Mobile page, destination 16958:291, 12 links (landing 10 including the in-frame menu layer, menu 2).
- Not linked on purpose: Understand our approach and Explore the Akines philosophy (not sign-in gated), the experience card links (Explore conversation, Explore wellbeing), step labels, Create. Explore Akines and Find my kind of connection are treated as member actions; say if either should go elsewhere.
- Cross-page jumps are impossible in Figma, which is why the sign-in frames live on the same page as each landing page.

Desktop sign-in flow (frames 1 Default 16956:149, 2 Focus 16956:223, 3 Missing 16956:289, 4 Invalid 16956:370, 5 Loading 16956:446, 6 Success 16956:514):
- 1 Default: click the email field goes to 2 Focus; Continue goes to 3 Missing (empty submit).
- 2 Focus: Continue or the Enter key goes to 3 Missing.
- 3 Missing: Continue or Enter goes to 4 Invalid; clicking the field goes back to 2 Focus.
- 4 Invalid: Continue or Enter goes to 5 Loading; clicking the field goes back to 2 Focus.
- 5 Loading: no control responds (no double submit); after 1.8 s it moves to 6 Success.
- 6 Success: Use a different email returns to 1 Default.
- The logo on all six returns to the landing page (16781:941). Figma prototypes cannot take typed text, so the second Continue stands in for typing an incomplete address and the third for correcting it.
- Interactive components on the Components page (frame Sign-in components): Social Button hovers, a click switches to Loading and it resets to Default after 2.4 s; Primary Button hover and press; Email Input hover; Account Prompt hover. These also play inside every sign-in frame.
- Tablet: the logo returns to the tablet landing page. Mobile: Continue goes to the error frame, clicking the field in the error frame returns to the default, the logo returns to the landing page.
- Flow starting points added: Sign-in (Desktop) 16956:149, Sign-in (Tablet) 16958:149, Sign-in (Mobile) 16958:291. The existing flows were left alone.
- Not wired: Create an account (there is no sign-up screen), the Google and Apple buttons beyond their loading state (no provider screens), tablet states beyond the default.

Tooling notes: (31) A navigate destination has to be a top-level frame (a child of the page or a section), so the sign-in frames were taken out of their label wrappers and board first. (32) setReactionsAsync takes the actions array: navigate is {type:'NODE', navigation:'NAVIGATE', destinationId, transition}; variant switching is navigation 'CHANGE_TO' with the variant id; keys are trigger {type:'ON_KEY_DOWN', device:'KEYBOARD', keyCodes:[13]}; AFTER_TIMEOUT takes seconds; read-back names hover-in triggers MOUSE_ENTER. (33) An instance keeps its own reactions and also reports the component's variant reactions (the Email input instance lists 2). (34) flowStartingPoints can read back as "Flow 1" right after the write; re-assign the name in a later call and read it back. (35) Before linking, reactions were scanned per button so nothing already wired was overwritten (none had a click reaction).

(41) To screenshot only the first screen of a very tall frame, clone it into a temporary clipped frame of viewport size, screenshot that, then delete it.

Request (10:00): "Redesign the login page to be a slide-out panel just like the akines menu", with the same blur as the menu. Decisions agreed with the user: it opens from the left like the menu; form only (Close row, hairline, logo, "Welcome back." heading, supporting line, email field, Continue, "or continue with", Google, Apple, "Create an account", and the tagline "Less scrolling. More belonging." at the bottom like the menu's brand block); the old full-page sign-in screens are kept as a reference.
- Look: copied from the menu. Page behind is black at 35% with a 24 background blur; panel is 460 wide on desktop (420 on tablet), 16 inset, radius 8, padding 24, gap 12, ivory (Surface/Akines/Primary/Strong, bound variable) at 90% with a 40 background blur. Mobile is an opaque ivory sheet with a top bar (close on the left, logo in the middle), like the mobile menu, so it has no blur.
- Components (Components page, new frame "Sign-in panel components" at the bottom, one section per device): component sets "Sign-in Overlay Desktop" 16992:3111 (1512 x 894 variants), "Sign-in Overlay Tablet" 16994:3609 (1194 x 746) and "Sign-in Overlay Mobile" 16995:4295 (402 x 768). Each has a State property: Default, Focus, Missing, Invalid, Loading, Success. A variant is the whole overlay (dim layer, backdrop, panel), because the backdrop has to reset the state when it closes the panel.
- Where the logo sits: under the hairline on desktop; at the right end of the Close row on tablet, because the worst state (wrapped validation message) would not fit the 714 px panel otherwise (it now leaves 12 px); in the top bar on mobile. Tablet and mobile use the 32 px heading and the 28 / 24 form gaps from the earlier tablet and mobile sign-in screens.
- Behaviour (all inside the component, via CHANGE_TO between the variants): click the email field goes to Focus; Continue goes Default or Focus to Missing, Missing to Invalid, Invalid to Loading; Enter does the same from Focus, Missing and Invalid; Loading goes to Success after 1.8 s; "Use a different email" goes back to Default; Close and the backdrop (desktop and tablet) set signin-open to false and reset to Default. The click targets are plain wrapper frames named "Email input (click)" and "Continue (click)" around the instances, so the instances keep their own hover states.
- Opening: new boolean variable signin-open (collection "Prototype state", default false, id VariableID:16990:149). Each long landing frame has a fixed child instance "Sign-in (prototype)" (before the Browser chrome bar; numberOfFixedChildren raised by one) whose visibility is bound to signin-open: Desktop 16781:941, 16863:2596 and 16863:2807, Tablet 16866:2094, Mobile 16877:149. Every control that used to navigate to the old sign-in screens in those five frames now sets signin-open to true (8 per frame: Sign in, Join Akines, the hero, Join the community and call-to-action buttons and two footer links). On mobile the Sign in and Join Akines buttons inside the mobile menu set menu-open to false and signin-open to true.
- Left as it was on purpose: the old sign-in frames (section 05, six desktop, two tablet, two mobile) and their flow starting points; the reference-only frames "Scrolled header / preview" and the two "Context / Hero (preview only)" frames still navigate to the old screens.
- Review sections on the canvas: "06 - AKINES / SIGN-IN PANEL (Desktop 1512)" (page Desktop, x 33200), "(Tablet 1194)" (Tablet page, x 7500) and "(Mobile 402)" (Mobile page, x 4200): six state frames each (the desktop and tablet ones sit over a copy of the hero so the blur shows), with annotations. These copies have no reactions; play the landing frames to test.
- Checked: screenshots of Default, Missing, Invalid and the six mobile states; the panel was also screenshotted open inside temporary clipped clones of the three landing frames (deleted afterwards). Variable default is false and every overlay is hidden; flow starting points unchanged; fixed-child counts 3 / 4 / 4 / 3 / 3. Not played in Present mode from the workspace.
- Known limits: like the menu, the panel appears instantly (the variable switch has no slide animation, and a Move in transition cannot be applied to a variable-driven layer). The Enter key and the Loading timer run on the variant itself; play them once to confirm Figma triggers them inside an overlay instance. "Create an account" and the Google and Apple buttons still go nowhere beyond their loading state.
- Tooling notes: (42) A reaction on a nested instance can only change that instance's own variant, so to switch the outer panel variant put the click on a plain wrapper frame around the instance. (43) createComponentFromNode on a clone keeps fills and variable bindings; unbind visibility with setBoundVariable('visible', null) first. (44) A temporary frame made with createFrame is 100 x 100 and clips; turn clipsContent off or size it before building inside it. (45) Text.fontSize needs the font loaded first, even on a cloned text layer.

### Create account flow redesign, proposed 1 Nov 2026 (04:45 Lagos), awaiting approval
The user sent six screenshots of the old join flow and asked for a breakdown of a simpler, faster flow that keeps the same structure and the editorial premium look. Nothing was built and Figma was not touched; this is a proposal to agree first.
- Old flow, 5 screens (progress bar of 5): (1) "Take a seat. Stay awhile." with name, email, mobile number ("One account per number"), date of birth (3 dropdowns), password with 12+ character rules, a community promises checkbox and "Create my account"; (2) "Check your inbox", a 6-digit code and "Confirm and continue", with "Send a new code" and "Use a different email"; (3) "Which chair are you sitting in?" with role chips (choose one), interest chips (pick any) and city, plus Back, Continue and "Skip this for now"; (4) "What brings you here today?" with nine photo cards, one choice, a "Six chairs. One conversation. Tonight." banner after choosing, then "Continue with this reason" and "Skip and go to community"; (5) "Welcome to Akines, Nde." with a first-step photo card, "Find a seat" and "Go to Home". Every screen has "Already a member? Sign in" at the top left. The old screens use a bold sans headline with an orange accent word and a teal button, which differs from the landing and sign-in panel (Alice headlines, black pill button, ivory, hairlines).
- Proposed flow, 4 screens: (1) Google or Apple in one tap, or first name + email, with one consent line instead of the checkbox; (2) email code that auto-submits on the sixth digit, accepts paste, has a resend countdown and a "wrong email?" link, and doubles as the sign-in method so there is no password; (3) "What brings you here today?" kept, but tapping a card chooses and moves on (no Continue button), Skip stays; (4) welcome kept almost as is. The "Which chair" screen is removed; city, interests and role are asked later when useful and as a "finish your profile" card on Home. Mobile number moves to just before the first Presence Room. Date of birth becomes a single "I'm 18 or over" line if policy allows.
- Look: same card on ivory, progress bar (4 segments), eyebrow, headline pattern, quiet Back and Skip links, photo cards; restyled into the landing and sign-in system. Desktop, tablet and mobile in Chrome windows like the sign-in screens, built on the existing pages as new sections.
- Open decisions for the user: (a) is the mobile number or date of birth a hard requirement at sign-up for legal or safety reasons; (b) drop the password for the email code, or keep an optional password in settings; (c) should steps 1 and 2 live in the same left slide-out panel (Join mode, so "Join Akines" and "Create an account" open it) with the photo-card step and welcome full-screen afterwards, or should the whole flow stay as full-page stepped cards; (d) whether a checkbox is legally required for consent (for the team or legal to confirm).
- User answers (1 Nov 2026, 04:55 Lagos): (1) design the desktop version only, on the Desktop page, until approved, then tablet and mobile; (2) sign-up should have one input that accepts email or another contact (wording unclear: either email or mobile number, or one email field shared by sign in and sign up; asked the user to confirm), and everything else is added later in the profile; (3) a username is required, like X and Instagram; (4) role chips, interest chips and city are not in sign-up, a "complete your profile" card appears after the first successful login; (5) "I'm 18 and over" is the age check, not a date of birth; (6) creating a password is important, so the password stays (this overrides the passwordless proposal). The user asked for confirmation of understanding before any design work.
